element state\n            ElementDynamic& elem_write = world.elements_dynamic_swap[i];\n            elem_write.current_energy = elem_dyn.current_energy + total_flux;\n }\n}\n\n// Validate simulation results\nbool validateResults(const World& world) {\n    val_t energy_sum = 0.0;\n    val_t flux_sum = 0.0;\n    val_t energy_max = Output matches the serial code exactly: the result hash, the `-r` results block and the `-v` validation text were identical in every run I compared. Only `unstructured.cpp` and `CMakeLists.txt` changed, and the executable is still `unstructured`.\n\n**How it works**\n- **Splitting the mesh:** the grid's rows are divided into contiguous blocks, one per rank. Each rank builds only its own rows plus one copy of its neighbours' edge rows above and below (the \"ghost\" rows). It keeps the original element structures and neighbour-index lookups, so it is still an unstructured-mesh kernel.\n- **Each iteration:** ranks swap only the energy values of their edge rows with neighbours (`MPI_Isend`/`MPI_Irecv`). While those messages are in flight, each rank updates its interior rows, then the edge rows once the data arrives. Neighbours are visited in the same order as before, so the floating-point results are bit-for-bit identical to the original.\n- **Hash:** each rank hashes its own elements using their global indices, and the pieces are combined with an XOR reduction. That gives exactly the original hash.\n- **`-v` / `-r`:** results are gathered to rank 0 (`MPI_Gatherv`) and checked or printed there in the original order, so the sums match exactly. This only happens when those flags are given.\n- **Timing and output:** timing is measured between barriers, and only rank 0 prints. Ranks with no rows (more ranks than grid rows) are handled.\n- **CMake:** it now uses `find_package(MPI REQUIRED)` and links `MPI::MPI_CXX`.\n\n**Testing:** I compared against the original for grid sizes 1, 2, 3, 5, 7 and 300 at 1 to 8 ranks (`-v -r`), and every output was identical. The large case (`-n 4096 -i 100`, cores pinned) gave hash `DEDCC216490F2000` at every rank count:\n\n| Ranks | Time |\n|---|---|\n| 1 | 19.7 s |\n| 8 | 8.0 s |\n| 32 | 2.6 s |\n| 128 | 1.1 s |\n| 256 | 1.3 s (hardware threads) |\n\nThe kernel is limited by memory bandwidth, so speedup levels off once memory bandwidth is saturated. The machine was shared (load around 7), so expect some noise in the times.\n\n**Single-rank speed:** one rank ran slower than the serial binary when the OS was free to move it between cores. When I pinned both to the same core and memory node, they ran in the same time (about 4.8 s for 30 iterations), so the gap came from where the process landed on the machine's two memory nodes, not from MPI overhead. I'd launch with `mpirun --bind-to core`; above 128 ranks use `--bind-to hwthread`, because the machine has only 128 physical cores and a 256-rank run bound to cores printed nothing."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":424,"cache_read_input_tokens":41098,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":424},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"c94dc225-c15c-418a-9794-0f259fe67a7a","uuid":"a7adb4b0-2974-442f-b703-c0f4e9a299b9","timestamp":"2026-10-03T21:22:10.762Z","request_id":"req_011Cfg1G5oAr9rzShYUTJMox"}
